Track

History

The history and mythology of one culture at a time, read together: who told each story, what they gained by it, and what the evidence says. Long sourced reports with disputes named, a self-test and a syllabus you can rerun.

2 courses · 157 reports · 8 planned
History · Course 1complete
Greek history and mythology

Bronze Age palaces to the closing of the Academy, myth read against history.

13 units written13 in all16.4 h of reading
History · Course 2complete
Roman history and mythology

The Palatine huts to the last emperor in the West, myth read against history.

14 units written14 in all17.6 h of reading

Why history and myth together

the method of the track
μῦθοςmythos

In Homer, simply what someone says with authority: a speech, a command, a tale. By the fifth century it had come to mean the story you cannot check.

λόγοςlogos

A word, a count, an account. By the same century it meant the argued explanation, the one that gives its reasons and invites you to test them.

The Greeks drew that line themselves. Herodotus calls his work a historiē, an inquiry, and still reports what the Persians and Egyptians told him. Thucydides warns that his history leaves out the mythōdes, the storylike, and will seem less pleasant for it (1.22). Plato expels the poets from his ideal city and then ends the Republic with a myth of his own.

The tidy story of a clean progress "from mythos to logos" (the title of Wilhelm Nestle's book of 1940) is now itself treated as a modern myth: the Greeks never stopped telling myths; their philosophers used them, their historians argued with them, and their cities were governed by them. So each course reads a culture's myths and its history together, asks who told each story and what they gained by it, and keeps the evidence in view. The aim is not to debunk the myths but to understand them as well as the history. Later courses point back to earlier ones where they touch: Rome to Greece's units 7 and 10, Persia to units 6 and 11, Egypt to units 1 and 7.

Planned

8 courses, in the intended order

The two civilisations that border the Greek story, then the northern and insular traditions, then East Asia. Open a course to see its scope and spine.

History · Egypt
Egyptian history and mythology

Three thousand years, thirty-one dynasties, and a religion the Greeks themselves treated as the oldest in the world. Predynastic unification, the Old Kingdom and the pyramids, the Middle and New Kingdoms, Akhenaten, Ramesses and the Sea Peoples, the Late Period and Persian rule, the Ptolemies you met in unit 7; then the cosmogonies of Heliopolis, Memphis and Hermopolis, the Osiris cycle, the solar journey, ma'at, the Book of the Dead and the afterlife, temple and priesthood, and how to read hieroglyphs enough to recognise a cartouche.

SpinePyramid Texts · Coffin Texts · Book of the Dead · the Shabaka Stone · Herodotus book 2 · Wilkinson, The Rise and Fall of Ancient Egypt · Assmann, The Search for God in Ancient Egypt

History · Persia
Persian history and mythology

The other side of units 6 and 11, told from Persepolis rather than Athens. Elam and the Medes, Cyrus and the Achaemenid empire, Darius and Xerxes as the Persians saw them, the satrapal system, Zoroaster and the Avesta, Ahura Mazda and the dualist cosmos, the Parthians, the Sasanians and their wars with Rome, the Zoroastrian apocalyptic, then the Shahnameh as the national epic that fused myth and history into one narrative of kings.

SpineBehistun inscription · the Avesta (Gathas, Yashts) · Herodotus · Xenophon, Cyropaedia · Firdausi, Shahnameh · Briant, From Cyrus to Alexander · Boyce, Zoroastrians

History · Norse
Viking and Norse history and mythology

The Scandinavian Iron Age and the Viking expansion, 793 to 1066 and beyond: raids, trade routes to Byzantium and Baghdad, the settlement of Iceland, Greenland and Vinland, the Danelaw, Normandy, the Rus, the conversion. Then the mythology as the two Eddas preserve it: Ymir and the world's making, Yggdrasil and the nine worlds, Odin, Thor, Loki, Freyja and the Vanir war, the death of Baldr, Ragnarok, the heroic legends of Sigurd and the Volsungs, and the problem of a pagan mythology written down by Christians two centuries late.

SpinePoetic Edda · Snorri, Prose Edda · Heimskringla · Saga of the Volsungs · Egil's Saga · Njal's Saga · Price, Children of Ash and Elm · Lindow, Norse Mythology

History · Celts
Celtic history and mythology

Hallstatt and La Tène, the Celtic expansion across Europe, the sack of Rome and Delphi, Gaul and Caesar's conquest, the druids as the Greeks and Romans described them, Roman Britain, then the two literary survivals: the Irish cycles (the Mythological Cycle and the Tuatha Dé Danann, the Ulster Cycle and Cú Chulainn, the Fenian Cycle, the Kings' Cycle) and the Welsh Mabinogion, with the question of how much is pre-Christian myth and how much medieval literature.

SpineCaesar, Gallic War · Lebor Gabála Érenn · Táin Bó Cúailnge · the Mabinogion · Cunliffe, The Ancient Celts · Koch, Celtic Culture

History · England
English history and mythology

From Roman Britain and the Anglo-Saxon settlement through Alfred, the Conquest, the Angevins, Magna Carta, the Wars of the Roses, the Tudors and the Reformation, the Civil War, and the making of the constitutional state. Alongside it the legendary matter: Beowulf and the Germanic inheritance, Bede's England, Geoffrey of Monmouth's invented history and the Arthurian cycle from Nennius to Malory, Robin Hood, the Green Man, and how England manufactured a national past.

SpineBeowulf · Bede · the Anglo-Saxon Chronicle · Geoffrey of Monmouth · Malory, Le Morte d'Arthur · Tombs, The English and Their History · Morris, The Anglo-Saxons

History · Scotland
Scottish history and mythology

Picts, Scots, Britons and Angles; Dál Riata and the kingdom of Alba; Macbeth as he was; the Wars of Independence, Wallace and Bruce; the Stewarts, the Reformation and Knox, the Union of Crowns and of Parliaments, the Jacobite risings, the Clearances, the Enlightenment. Then the mythology and legend: the Pictish stones, the Celtic saints (Columba, Iona), the Fenian material in Gaelic Scotland, the kelpies, selkies and the Sìth, the Border ballads, Ossian and the eighteenth-century invention of Highland tradition.

SpineAdomnán, Life of Columba · the Declaration of Arbroath · Barbour, The Brus · the Child Ballads · Macpherson's Ossian · Devine, The Scottish Nation · Fraser, From Caledonia to Pictland

History · China
Chinese history and mythology

The Shang and the oracle bones, the Zhou and the Mandate of Heaven, the Warring States and the hundred schools, Qin unification, the Han, the period of division, Tang, Song, Yuan, Ming and Qing down to 1911. Then the mythology, which China kept largely in philosophy and ritual rather than epic: Pangu and Nüwa, the Three Sovereigns and Five Emperors, Yu and the flood, the Queen Mother of the West, the Jade Emperor and the bureaucratic heaven, Daoist immortals, Buddhist arrivals, and the great vernacular novels as a late mythology (Journey to the West, Investiture of the Gods).

SpineShijing · Shangshu · Sima Qian, Records of the Grand Historian · Shanhaijing · Zhuangzi · Journey to the West · Lewis, The Early Chinese Empires · Birrell, Chinese Mythology

History · Japan
Japanese history and mythology

Jōmon and Yayoi, the Yamato state and the Chinese model, Nara and Heian court culture, the rise of the warriors, Kamakura, the Mongol invasions, the Sengoku wars and unification, Tokugawa isolation, Meiji. Then the mythology as the Kojiki and Nihon Shoki set it down to legitimise the imperial house: Izanagi and Izanami, Amaterasu and the cave, Susanoo and the serpent, the descent of Ninigi, Jimmu; Shinto as practice, kami and shrines, the Buddhist synthesis, the yōkai tradition, and the Tale of the Heike as a national epic of defeat.

SpineKojiki · Nihon Shoki · Man'yōshū · Tale of Genji · Tale of the Heike · Totman, A History of Japan · Breen and Teeuwen, A New History of Shinto

How every course works

the constants
  1. One reader. Every course is written for the same person and read in order, on a phone, with the citations checked.
  2. Sources first. A unit names its sources and says where they disagree; a claim that cannot be sourced is marked unsourced or left out.
  3. One unit per chat, built in full. Each unit is written from a published brief or prompt, in its own chat, with its closing artefact: a self-test and carry-forward ideas for the history courses, a shipped deliverable for Brand + AI.
  4. Audited every four units. After every four units written in a hub, a stratified sample of claims is checked against its sources with a second model; a unit that fails gets a full audit. The model that wrote each unit is recorded.
  5. An open syllabus. Every prompt and brief is published with its course, so any unit can be regenerated, extended or checked.

CHANGELOG · 58 sessions since 22 October 2026

Changelog

Everything that has happened to Personal Mythology and, from session 13, to Academy, from the first request on 22 October 2026 to now, newest first. Each entry is one working session. It is assembled from the kit's own records (provenance.json, the review folders and the maintainer log) and from the session transcripts. Where a session recorded the site's version number, it is given. The exact day of each step in the first, three-day session was not recorded; its entries are in order.

4 November 2026Session 58Every contour drawing in slow motion, topographies on the course homes and What's new, the tile across the site (P93)

P93 ran in one chat on Opus 5.5 from 15:55 on 4 November, on Pedro's decision of 15:43, made on P92's home ("Can we animate that topographical illustration, very slow, expanding and rotating movements, across the board, and expand that to other pages, and make this new tile style propagate across the site as well as this new topo illustration? It looks freaking awesome!!!"). The clone was at 0cb6dd5; the permission check did not refuse the repo's scripts. Baseline build.py and verify.py: ALL CHECKS PASSED; the build changed the README size table and the shell's Backlog page (P93's queued row), committed as 77a902a. The shots of the start are reviews/p93/before/ (the home, What's new and the five course homes at 320, 360, 390, 430, 768 and 1280 px, dark and light, device scale 2, from a copy of the baseline so the work could go on; Rome at 320, 360 and 430 px with verify's own page setup, since --shots took the second history course only at three widths). No unit and no history report's text changed. Greece's course home changed, so the Greece edition was built, verified and republished (below).

  • The move (step 2). P92's 8 x 6 px drift (topo-drift) is gone. Every contour drawing, the home's hero, every tile's patch and every new topography, makes one slow move where motion is welcome: topo-turn, a turn from -3 to +3 degrees and a growth from 1 to 1.06 about its centre over 120 s each way, ease-in-out, alternating, transform only (theme tokens --topo-turn, --topo-grow, --topo-cycle); each drawing of a page starts at its own point of the cycle (--ph, from build.py's phases: k/n of the way in a stride order, so neighbours differ by more than 1/n); under reduced motion nothing moves on any page. .topo is a size container, so each layout's overscan is computed on its own frame, half of 100cqh * sin(turn) each side and half of 100cqw * sin(turn) above and below, plus a pixel; a patch's drawing fits its frame (meet, no overscan), its rings kept clear of the frame at every pose (turned_inside: each box turned and grown to both ends of the move). Every text over a topography is measured at five poses (the two ends of the sweep, its quarters and its middle; on the home with each area word in turn); --topo-a came down from .2 to .16 in both themes by the row's rule: at .2 a muted lede over two rings passing within a pixel measured 4.11 to 4.48:1 in the light theme.
  • The topographies (step 3). Each course home's hero carries one (hero_layer): one rise per unit in its order (a history course's syllabus units, the audit unit left out, as its tile's patch), a written unit by its words, a planned unit a dashed ring, a contour every 5.5th of its largest unit; a tall layout (a square) below 1100 px and a wide one (2:1) from it, its rises on a serpentine lattice (ct_lattice) and sized (ct_fit) so each written rise closes min(2, its levels) rings of its own and stands, turned and grown to both ends of the move, inside the window a 390 px phone, the 768 px tablet and the 1280 px desktop all show, above the fade. The layer runs from the top bar to the hero's foot, the full width beside the sidebar (.hero.has-topo); the hero keeps its own height, because it holds the stats and the buttons, whose boxes are opaque, so the lines pass under them. What's new's hero carries one rise per day it lists, oldest at the left along a ridge (ct_ridge), its height the words filed that day, a contour every 11,000 words (NEW_INTERVAL: 22 Oct, 221,673 words, stands 20 levels; 30 Oct, 14,178, 1.29, a ring of its own), each day in the tone of the course that filed the most words that day; its layouts 1.6:1 and 3.7:1 for its flatter hero. The Changelog, the Backlog, the Figure kinds page and the reading pages have none. Layers, as verify measures them: the home 14.3 KB, Greece 8.3, Rome 9.8, Personal brand 10.9, Building with AI 10.0, Information architecture 8.3, What's new 11.0 (every one aria-hidden and textless, each at most 20 KB). The shell would have been 945 KB with the markdown course homes' tiles and layers in it, 79 KB short of the limit with eight IA units to come, so the markdown course homes are fetched files now, as the history homes are (<course>/home.html); the shell is 789 KB.
  • The tile (step 4). One builder, tile(), for every course, unit and planned course: the chip with the status pill under it, the title, a two-line line, three stats, its own patch (patch_block). A history unit's tile (unit_tile, replacing ucard) is the summary of a disclosure that opens to its module rows (its stats modules written, in all, hours; its patch its modules by their words; opened, its line whole); a markdown unit's (in mdcourse.py) is the link to its reading page, or to its brief while not written (.pending, one dashed ring; its stats sections, words, hours; its patch its sections by their words); each of History's eight planned courses is a ghost (.ghost: the chip outlined, the title, its scope in two lines, no stats, one dashed ring, opening to its spine). Two columns from 768 px, a lone tile half the width on every page; while a unit tile is open its grid's tiles take their own heights. The unit cards' and the planned rows' classes are gone (.ucard, .ucards, .uch, .uwhy, .ncard, details.course.soon, .soonlist).
  • Theme 6.6 (step 5). 4i: the move, the overscan, .hero.has-topo; 4h: the tile everywhere (the home-only rules now hold for every tile), its disclosure, ghost and pending forms; 6: .cgrid for every list of tiles; 7: the tile's slack above its stats; 4a, 4b, 4c: the eyebrow's course label and a pill's word their tone mixed 78% with --fg in both themes (the dark blue label measured 4.31:1 on the page), a plain pill in --muted (in --dim about 3.5:1 on a dark card), a filled button's label at full strength in both themes (black at .85 gave 4.1:1 on the dark blue), breadcrumb separators in --muted (3.7:1 in --dim). contrast.py measures the new pairs in both themes (reviews/p93/contrast.txt: all pass).
  • Found and fixed on the way. A markdown unit's words counted its sections' next and previous buttons, their band numerals and the token steps"> of the report's opening tag (build and verify alike); a unit's words are now its sections' own text (mdcourse.section_text), about 3% fewer (Personal brand's hours 5.0 to 4.8, Building with AI's 5.5 to 5.3). The Greece edition had not built since P92 (its HERO_AT, in the full Academy's area order, placed four rises for one area); HERO_AT is keyed by area. verify.py --edition greece could not have passed since P92 either (its home check hard-coded the full Academy's Planned list and site links); it takes what an edition omits. A rise's levels were read from the field at its nearest grid node, so a rise barely over a level lost it and one unit could count differently in two layouts; each peak stands on a grid node and a rise's levels are its own words over the interval.
  • Checks (step 6). verify.py adds check_topo (every layer against the sources, aria-hidden, textless, at most 20 KB, one layout shown, the own rings, every rise in view at 390 px and up and above the fade, the frame covered by the turned layout, every patch's lines inside its frame, no topography on the pages that have none; with motion only transform and opacity, every drawing moving at its own phase; under reduced motion none), check_tiles (one class set, the fields, each patch against its sources, a history unit's rows, a markdown unit's link, the ghosts, the grids, nothing past an edge) and texts_contrast (every hero text over a topography against the pixels under it at five poses), at six widths, dark and light, on the home, the five course homes, What's new, the Changelog, the Backlog, the Figure kinds page and one reading page per course; LCP and the longest task at 390 px with motion allowed on the home, Greece's home and What's new; --shots takes the course homes and What's new at every width and the motion sheets (the layer's own frame at five poses) of the home, Greece and Information architecture. On the way its own faults were found and fixed: a pose paused the theme switch's colour transitions half-way (a transition outranks even an !important rule), a color-mix() colour computes as color(srgb …) in 0 to 1, a short phone's screen ended above the hero's foot, and a clamped line computes as flow-root in this Chromium.
  • Results. verify.py on the published build: ALL CHECKS PASSED (reviews/p93/verify-after.txt); verify.py --edition greece: ALL CHECKS PASSED; contrast.py: every pair passes (reviews/p93/contrast.txt). LCP at 390 px with motion, on a fresh load from the local server: the home 124 ms, Greece 164 ms, What's new 144 ms; the longest task 0 ms, no task over 50 ms. Shell 789 KB (807,802 bytes), 205 fetched files. The after shots and motion sheets: reviews/p93/after/.
  • Found by the checks and fixed. At 320 px a chip that would not wrap ("Information architecture · DD-IA1/2/3") widened its grid's one column 11 px past the grid: a tile grid's columns are minmax(0, 1fr) and the chip breaks between its two parts where it does not fit; verify.py checks every tile against its grid. A tile's stats are three columns each its content's width, spread across the tile, so "modules written" keeps one line where it fits. contrast.py prints the two-lines-stacked column as a record, not a bar (the built page, at five poses, is the authority).
  • The checkers (step 7). Fresh Opus 5.5 agents (claude-opus-5-5 in every transcript), each given only its folder, their reports verbatim in reviews/p93/ (check-motion-1.md, -2; check-tiles-1.md to -4). Motion and topographies: FIX (63 calls; an error: 30 Oct stood flat in the tall layout while it held a level in the wide, its levels read from the field at the nearest grid node: each peak now stands on a node and a rise's levels are its own words over the interval), then PASS (71). Tiles: FIX (78; an error: a third line showed under the clamp in a tile stretched beside a taller one, so the slack goes above the stats; should-fix: the plain pill in --dim, now --muted; the stats wrapping, now three columns), FIX (64; should-fix: a section under one contour level drew no ring, so it takes a spot ring, .ct-spot, and the record carries each tile's modifiers, which verify checks), FIX (41; should-fix: the grid overrun above), PASS (62; three minors, all fixed: the stats' columns, a comment that said "row" for "grid", the record column). Transcript checks: Read, Glob and Grep only and every path inside the folder in all six, but for one Read by the third tile checker of the harness's saved copy of its own Grep over its folder's data-extract.json (the output was too large to return inline; its content is the folder's file). Settled with reason, not changed: the home's User Experience rise at a 390 px phone's poses .75 and 1, and the home's rises at 768 and 1280 px (P92's places, given); the dark blue lines faint at --topo-a .16 (the row lowers --topo-a for contrast); the own-rings rule is for the heroes, not for a patch (a patch marks a small part with a spot ring); two lines stacked in one pixel (the five poses on the built page are the row's measure, and nothing measured nears the bar); a reading page's section numeral at 4.29:1 (30 px, large text, 3:1).
  • Choices taken. The turn and the growth are one move (from -3 degrees at 1 to +3 at 1.06), so a drawing is smallest at one end of its sweep; phases in a stride order, so no two neighbours start together. The overscan is computed per frame from the tokens by size containers, not a fixed margin, so a tall layout and a wide one each get exactly what the turn needs; a patch has no overscan (its frame is inside a tile), its rings kept clear of the frame at every pose instead. A course home's hero keeps its own height and its topography runs under the stats and buttons, whose boxes are opaque. A history course's topography, like its tile's patch on the home, leaves out the audit unit (a review, not a unit of reading); on the course home the audit unit keeps its own tile, pending, its modules flat rings. What's new: a contour every 11,000 words, fixed, so 22 Oct stands 20 levels and the smallest day (30 Oct) one; each day in the tone of the course that filed the most words that day. A markdown unit not yet written links to its brief. The markdown course homes are fetched files.
  • Published. Academy published once from this build, on the artifact read first (never a new one, never forced), then deploy.sh to Pages; the Greece edition republished on its artifact, read first. The identifiers follow in the record.
4 November 2026Session 57The home's hero: a topography from the data, the areas cycling, a quiet What's new (P92)

P92 ran in one chat on Opus 5.5 from 13:24 on 4 November, on Pedro's decisions of 13:16 (P91's shelf "looks awful [...] Such a long scroll in mobile, makes no sense. Let's go back"; after research into 2026 heroes: type first, the action in view, motion beside the headline rather than heavy motion behind it, a reduced-motion fallback for every animation). The clone was at 29c376d; the permission check did not refuse the repo's scripts. Baseline build.py and verify.py: ALL CHECKS PASSED; the build changed the README size table and the shell's Backlog and Changelog pages (P92's queued row), committed as f155586. The home shots of the start are reviews/p92/before/ (320, 360, 390, 430, 768 and 1280 px, dark and light, scaled to device scale 2). No unit and no history report's text changed, so the Greece edition is not republished (it builds).

  • The hero (step 2). "Academy"; under it the four areas of knowledge (the THEMES names in order, each in its tone) one at a time, three seconds each, cross-fading, by CSS keyframes on stacked words (opacity and transform, no script, no link or focus stop), aria-hidden; one static line naming all four in their tones for a screen reader (its dots aria-hidden, a comma for the pause), the line shown under reduced motion, when nothing moves; the lede as it was; a quiet outlined link, "What's new · IA7 · 3 Nov" (the first of dated_units), 44 px, to #new. Behind it the topography: viz.py's new home kind contours (P91's shelf, its keys, classes and checks gone), declared by build.py (HERO_TOPO): one rise per area, its height the words written in its live courses (History 510,493, Marketing 74,284, Technology 82,023, User Experience 66,866), a contour every 22,000 words (HERO_INTERVAL: 23 levels, 3, 3, 3), in two fixed layouts (HERO_AT: tall below 1100 px, every rise inside a 390 px phone's view; wide from 1100 px, across the full width beside the sidebar), each small rise closing two rings of its own; the field a sum of smooth bumps (a Gaussian whose outline two angular harmonics push in and out, phases from the label, so every build draws the same) sampled on a grid, contour lines by marching squares, simplified (Ramer-Douglas-Peucker) and written in whole-unit relative steps, each stretch of line in the tone of the rise that dominates it; aria-hidden, no text, 14,016 bytes. It drifts 8 x 6 px over 30 s by a CSS transform where motion is welcome, is still otherwise, and fades over the hero's last 10%. The hero is 70svh tall, its copy in the lower part with the contours above it, so on a phone the first area's heading peeks under it; it bleeds to 100cqw (the body is an inline-size container: the width beside the sidebar less any scrollbar) with its copy in the column. No totals, no shelf, no Continue, no map.
  • The areas (step 3). The four sections in THEMES order; from 768 px the header holds the "Track" label, the name, the lede and the count with its rule, on a phone the name only, closed by the first tile's rule; one tile per live course with the same fields (the chip with the pill under it, the title, the two-line tile, the three stats) and its own static contour patch (tile_patch: one rise per unit in course order, a written unit by its words, a planned unit a dashed ring, a contour every 5.5th part of the course's largest unit, 4:1, the tile's width), the whole tile the link. History's two folded blocks stay in its section; the constants, the site links, New since and its one link as P91 left them. Hours of reading take one decimal everywhere (16.4, 5.0), on the tiles and the course homes.

P68 · the reading layout, every unit and report of every course. One rule in the renderer (mdcourse.reading, called by mdcourse.py for the markdown courses and by build.py for the history reports; stepping in shell.js; theme section 9), no page-level edit:

  • Content first. The page opens on the title and the one-line scope (the unit's units_data.py scope; a history report's syllabus line), then section 1. The provenance paragraph that used to open a markdown unit, the old section chips and the history reports' "Written with" line and prompt are gone from the top.
  • One section at a time. A sticky bar under the top bar: "Section n of N" over the section's title, a progress bar, and the section menu (every section, then Sources, How this unit was made and the "Whole unit" toggle). Each section ends on Previous and Next; the last ends on "End of the unit". The section last read is kept per page, so Continue, a drawer item or a reload reopens it there; a deep link to a section (#m54-3, #read-ia2-2) or to an accordion (#m54-sources, also on a fresh load) shows it first.   • Process last. After the last section: Sources (a markdown unit's Sources list; a history report's curated bibliography, bibliography/<course>/r-<id>.md), then "How this unit was made" (the provenance paragraph or "Written with" line, the writer's unsure list, the fresh-check line, taken out of the unsure list where the writer put it there, a history report's checks and audits with their verdicts, and the brief or prompt with its copy button), both closed. The next-unit cards come after them and now say "Previous unit" and "Next unit".
  • Text that stands before a unit's first heading (DD-B3's and B4's openings, for example) is the unit's own text and opens section 1, before its band.
  • Wide tables get the figures' fade and a "scroll sideways for the whole table" cue. The top bar is more opaque (0.94), so text no longer shows through it.

30 October 2026Session 31The figure renderer's drawing faults, and a flaky check (P55, P56)

Backlog rows P55 and P56 of docs/BACKLOG.md, Opus 5.5. viz.py now draws the cases the N4 checkers met and worked around (reviews/p43-rome-notes.md), under one rule: a label that cannot fit moves, or the drawing widens and scrolls; it is never clipped, shrunk or cut, and a block the renderer cannot draw honestly stops the build saying what to change. Timeline: a span's label sits inside its bar only when it fits with 6 units either side, else beside the bar in the muted column-head mono; spans sit in lanes, so spans that overlap in time never share one (abutting spans do); the drawing widens until span, event and tick labels fit, and stops the build past 2,400 units naming the ticks too close to label both; ticks, events and spans outside start..end stop it. A year 0 anywhere (tick, event, value) stops the build: there is no year 0. Compare and bars: labels wrap in a measured column up to 240 wide (compare's was a fixed 170 that clipped; bars' wrapped at 160); compare's values take thousands separators; a unit of three letters or more prints after a space ("1,200 men"), in every kind. Tree: boxes keep 112 units (wider when one word needs it), labels wrap whole and a level grows to its longest label; a tree more than 15 % over 700 scrolls instead of shrinking. Objects: the letter-by-letter wrap was attrs given as a bare string; any list field given a string now stops the build, in every kind. Range: "years": true prints years; negative whole numbers without it stop the build. Contracts recorded in viz.py's docstring. verify.py: the figure checks (nothing outside the drawing, text inside its box or bar, no two boxes or labels overlapping, no free label over a box) now cover every kind, the A8 and data kinds included, and run on every figure of every panel in the phone pass (246 figures on 74 panels) as well as the examples page, which gains eight hard cases (one per fault) and lists the four blocks that must stop the build, each checked. P56: a fresh-load anchor is measured once its top has held still in the landing band (up to 3 s), with one retry; the same for every anchor in that loop and for #rm12-2. Figure data unchanged but one: rome/r-2.4.html's tick at 0, which drew "AD 0" and now stops the build, moved to 1 (AD 1), as the new message says; no prose, word count or provenance changed. 77 existing figures draw differently (26 compare, 35 timeline, 14 tree, 1 range, plus 2.4's tick), all looked at whole at natural size, the named pages at 390 px and desktop. 29 October 2026Session 27Data figures, and figures on a phone (P44)

Backlog row N1 (P44) of docs/BACKLOG.md, Opus 5.5, run unattended overnight. viz.py draws four more kinds for data, each with its contract in the docstring: line (named series over an ordered x, numbers to scale or labels evenly, years with yr; every point labelled with its value or only the ends; dashed annotations with a note; a legend), bars (grouped or stacked horizontal bars, values at the bar ends, a stacked segment's value inside it when it fits, a legend), scatter (labelled points on two numeric axes with gridlines, optional quadrant lines and names; each label takes the nearest free spot and a label with none stops the build) and range (per row a low-to-high bar, an optional point estimate, each end's value with its source tag), and matrix gains a numeric mode (numbers shaded in five steps of one tone, a scale legend). Point labels, legend entries, quadrant names and range tags sit on plates, so the box checks also prove they do not overlap. Theme 5.4: the data classes in §4i, and the phone rule: below 768 px a figure draws at its natural size, one SVG unit to one CSS pixel, and scrolls sideways inside its frame, never the page; shell.js fades the edge that has more drawing and shows "scroll sideways for the whole figure" under a figure only when it is wider than its frame (on load, on resize, when a fetched panel lands and when a <details> opens). The cue is added by the script, so no report's word count moves. The line chart's figure class is viz-linechart, because viz-line already styles structural lines.

Backlog row A2 of docs/academy.md, Opus 5.5. The single page was 4.9 MB against a 16 MB cap with eight history courses still to come. No report changed and no flag was cleared.

The build

build.py writes academy/: index.html, the shell (home, drawer, course homes, syllabi, build pages, changelog, backlog), and one file per report, academy/<course>/r-<id>.html for Greece and Rome and academy/brand-<k>/read-<code>.html for a Brand + AI reading page. shell.js fetches a report the first time it is opened, keeps it in the panel for the rest of the visit, builds its pager once it has landed, and fetches the next report in the course in the background. A section link into an unopened report (#m23-2) loads the report, then scrolls to the section. A failed fetch shows the report's title, the reason and a Try again button. 27 October 2026Session 10Bibliographies for every report; the Greek Opus cohort; the overlap checks

Three parts, two models. The curated bibliographies ran on Sonnet 5.5 (agents, Pedro's choice for cost); the review ran on Fable 5.1. No report was written.

Curated bibliographies, all 137 reports

One Sonnet 5.5 agent per unit group (26 groups, about 4.5 million tokens) wrote bibliography/<course>/r-<id>.md for every existing report to bibliography/BIBLIO-BRIEF.md: ancient sources with the passages the report cites, modern works completed to full references, corpora, and a Not resolved list. Web verification was thin (search returned links without text; Crossref blocked), so most modern references completed from memory are marked "(not verified)", "(partly verified)" or "(identified from context)". The files are an artefacts entry in provenance.json, flagged until Fable 5.1 spot-checks about three units. The agents also reported five apparent slips in the reports; three were real and are fixed (Rome 1.5 credited The Genesis of Roman Architecture to three authors, it is Hopkins alone; Greek 1.1 gave Hoeck's Kreta the wrong title and year; Greek 10.2 credited Schliemann of Troy to Calder and Traill, it is Traill), two were not (Rome 6.3's Dobbin 1995 and 10.3's Paul Davies are right; those bibliography files were corrected). Rome 4.2's unverified "(2014)" after William Murray, added in session 9, was removed.

Greek 13.2, 13.5 and 13.4: the Opus cohort

Six-report sample (10.4, 10.9, 11.3, 11.7, 12.1, 12.6), one fresh Fable agent each on the Greek scorecard (audits/AUDIT-BRIEF-GREECE.md): 4.0, 4.5, 4.5, 4.1, 4.8, 4.8. The stratum mean of 4.44 clears the rule, but the two provenances inside the sample differ by half a point (unit 12 with checkers 4.8; units 10 and 11 without, 4.28), the gap sits in one dimension (dispute boxes with unnamed sides, 10.4 and 11.7 at 2 and 3), and a mechanical scan found the same pattern across the unsampled units-10-to-11 reports and almost none in unit 12. The stratum was split (audits/greece-13.4-cohorts.md): unit 12's six unsampled reports cleared "(sampled)"; the eleven of units 10 and 11 audited in full. Done: 10.7 (4.0), 10.8 (4.1), 10.10 (4.25), 10.11 (4.6), 10.12 (3.75, two slips, the lowest score of the review). Every erratum and revise item was applied as exact replacements under 13.5 (named sides for about thirty dispute boxes; titles for about forty modern works; six small factual slips; one new box on Rome's adoption of Aeneas in 10.12), check.py OK on each, and the eleven became "Opus 5.5, reviewed with Fable 5.1". The audits of 11.1, 11.2, 11.4 were cut off by an API spend limit and 11.5, 11.6, 11.8 not started; those six remain flagged.

Rome 14.3 and Greek 13.3: the overlap checks

Mechanical first (audits/overlap.py, seven-word shingles and a name-Jaccard over every written pair the syllabi list: 42 Rome, 38 Greek): verbatim overlap is nil everywhere, so the question was semantic. Readers (one agent per group, audits/OVERLAP-BRIEF.md) then classified the later report section by section for the Rome deep-dive pairs (11.1; 13.1 and 13.2 with their Greek cross-course pairs), the seven unmeasured Rome core pairings and the six Greek pairs with the highest signal: every one of the 22 pairs passed (Rome 90 to 100 percent new; Greek 70 to 95 percent new or deeper), with 4.4 → 10.7 at the threshold. 27 October 2026Session 9Every Rome report reviewed: the sample, the capstone, the decision

The second session on Fable 5.1. Rome 14.2, 14.5 and 14.4 were run; all 59 Rome reports are off the review queue. No report was written.

The review alert

  • review_due.py was added at Pedro's request: it counts the complete units (both courses pooled) that still hold a flagged report and prints "FABLE REVIEW DUE" at four. register.py and build.py print its line after every registration and build, FABLE-REVIEW.md opens with it, and BACKLOG.md tells a writing session to stop at the unit boundary and say so in its first line when it fires. Tested on simulated states (three units: status line; four: due).

Rome 14.2, twelve reports by stratified sample

  • The 58 unaudited reports were split by how they had been checked: quality-pass "keep" (13), quality-pass "fix" (26) and the fresh-checker pipeline (17), the capstone kept apart. Sample: 0.1, 1.4, 1.6, 4.2, 7.3; 2.3, 3.5, 5.2, 6.4; 8.3, 9.2, 11.2. Each was audited by a fresh agent on the Rome scorecard with the brief in audits/AUDIT-BRIEF.md: whole report read, at least fifteen claims checked (29 to 35 in practice), every pointer opened, every self-test answered, each writer's unsure list and each earlier review's open items taken as the starting point. Scorecards in audits/rome-14.2-r-<id>.md.
  • Scores: 0.1 4.9, 1.4 4.9, 1.6 4.8, 4.2 4.4, 7.3 4.8, 2.3 4.6, 3.5 4.6, 5.2 4.8, 6.4 4.6, 8.3 4.9, 9.2 4.6, 11.2 4.7; mean 4.72. Verdicts 8 pass, 4 revise, 0 rewrite. Four factual slips in 36,459 words: 1.4's "the literature begins about 200 BC"; 2.3's Pliny "thought Cornelia innocent" (he withholds judgement); 4.2's "more than 20,000" mercenaries (Polybius: about 20,000); 11.2's Sulla letter dated 83 (written from the East in 85 or 84). Thirty-seven errata rows in all, the rest citation sharpenings (a title and date for a modern work, a chapter for a book), two dispute boxes given their unnamed side (0.1 Timpe, 4.2 Thiel and Goldsworthy), a loose pointer (3.5 to 4.3), one place-name form (Acragas for Agrigentum) and three self-test answers brought into line with their bodies.
  • 14.5: every erratum and revise item applied as an exact replacement, check.py OK on each (all twelve inside the band), and each entry in provenance.json set to "Opus 5.5, reviewed with Fable 5.1".
  • The 3.5 auditor found the sides reversed in audits/rome-14.6-syllabus.md's coinage row (Burnett and HN Italy hold the early date, Crawford's RRC the late one); corrected there.

Rome 14.4, the decision

  • audits/rome-14.4-cohorts.md. Against the Greek Fable baseline (mean 4.8, spread 4.6 to 4.9) the Rome sample's mean of the eight shared dimensions is 4.68 against 4.78, inside the baseline's own spread: noise. One systematic habit: modern works cited by surname and year with no title (nine of twelve scored 4 on citation for it). Rome's length discipline is better (+12 percent on average, all in band; the Greek core +19 with two out of band). Not found: Livy over-trusted, Greek modules retold, weaker self-tests or argument. Rome reads as if the same author wrote it.
  • Strata: 4.76, 4.65 and 4.73, no report with more than one factual error, so all three met the rule fixed on 27 October (mean at least 4.4, at most one factual error per report). The 44 unsampled reports became "Opus 5.5, reviewed with Fable 5.1 (sampled)".
  • Applied with the clearance: the review_notes corrections to 3.1 (Livy's sources "besides Fabius Pictor") and 6.1 (Judge's reading and the aureus of 28), and 4.1's treaty box now names Beaumont, as 1.6 does, not Walbank.

Rome 10.5, the capstone

  • Audited on its own: all 40 model answers read against their home reports (including the twelve corrected today), 35 claims checked directly, every pointer opened. 4.7, pass, no factual error; two gap-note pointers tidied (gap 7 now names Ardashir and module 7.4; gap 1 says 11.3 fills its part "once written"). The auditor's one structural note, that six Part A questions reuse core self-tests, is left for the maintainer. Off the queue: no Rome report is flagged.

Greek corrections applied (Fable-written, no flag involved)

  • 7.2's Epicurean gods box (Konstan realist; Long and Sedley idealist); 7.3's Veii statuettes "of disputed date" and the 88 BC massacre figures (80,000 in Valerius Maximus and Memnon, 150,000 in Plutarch); 9.3's Ovide moralisé early fourteenth-century; 9.4's exam allowance "about four and a quarter hours"; 6.3's 3,000 prisoners credited to Plutarch; 8.1's Q8 answer. 7.2 and 8.1 still fail check.py on length, as before.

Two rules from Pedro, afternoon of 27 October

  • Length is a guide, not a law. A topic takes the words it needs, 2,000 or 6,000. check.py now reports the count against the target as a note and never fails on it; AGENT-SPEC.md, WRITER-BRIEF.md and the rubric (dimension 8 no longer scores length; padding is the fault) say so. On the new rule Greek 2.1 is a 5 on style and the baseline mean 4.9; no verdict in this session changes.
  • Every unit keeps its sources and bibliography for future checks. New biblio.py, run by build.py, extracts from every report's inline citations a per-unit list (bibliography/<course>/unit-<n>.md, 26 files covering all 137 reports): ancient works with locators, authors cited by bare locator, modern works with title and year, corpora and inscriptions, per report. Machine-made, so a check aid. Every new report's writer now also leaves a curated bibliography/<course>/r-<id>.md (WRITER-BRIEF.md); the unit file links to it. Curated files for the 137 existing reports were then written the same day by Sonnet 5.5 agents, one per unit group, to bibliography/BIBLIO-BRIEF.md; they are flagged in provenance.json artefacts until Fable 5.1 spot-checks about three units.

Kit

  • New: review_due.py, biblio.py and bibliography/, audits/AUDIT-BRIEF.md, thirteen scorecards, audits/rome-14.4-cohorts.md. README.md, BACKLOG.md, AGENT-SPEC.md updated for the alert; NEXT-SESSION.md rewritten to resume at Greek 13.2.

The quality pass

  • All 39 Rome reports were read by ten fresh reviewer agents, three to five reports each, grouped by unit. None had written or seen the reports.
  • Verdicts: 13 keep, 26 fix, none to rewrite. Findings: 41 errors, 62 should-fix and 106 minor. Each was checked before it was applied (reviews/2026-quality-pass/).
  • The recurring slips became rules in AGENT-SPEC.md:
    • a modern book's year or title not matching the edition named;
    • an ancient author cited for what only a later one says;
    • a Self-test answer contradicting a corrected body;
    • a dispute box with one side unnamed;
    • a figure without a source.
  • crosscheck.py greece found seven pairs of Greek reports with shared passages and six disputes told in several reports. They were logged for the Fable review.

Published, then planned

  • The site and both syllabi were published.
  • A re-check of the site found two bugs (fixed in session 6). NEXT-SESSION.md was rewritten for Rome units 8 to 13 and Greek unit 12, with a lane note for each module listing what the written reports already hand to it.

22 to 24 October 2026Session 1The Greek course, then Personal Mythology

It began on 22 October with a request for a syllabus to learn Greek history and mythology in depth, with a prompt for each topic.

Hellas: the syllabus and the core

  • The syllabus, 22 October: ten units and 50 modules, each with a one-line reason and a drop-in prompt, published as its own page.
  • Units 0 to 2: twelve reports written in parallel and assembled into one tabbed page.
  • Units 3 to 6: 37 reports, 114,729 words.
  • Unit 7: 41 reports, 126,137 words.
  • Units 8 and 9: ending with the capstone exam, 9.4. This finished the core's 50 reports.

The site takes shape

  • Mobile redesign: a fixed top bar with prev and next, and a full-height slide-out drawer grouped by unit, its units closed by default.
  • Light mode with a sun and moon toggle.
  • 23 October: the site opens on Home, with a Continue button. A Syllabus page shows every prompt with a copy button and a link to its report.
  • The home page restructured:
    • a to-scale timeline from 3000 BC to AD 529;
    • four parts (ground rules, myths, history, practice and legacy) plus the deep dives;
    • collapsible unit cards with progress bars;
    • "soon" rows in the drawer.
    • A drawer bug that filed 10.1 to 10.6 under unit 1 was fixed.
  • A "Next steps" page listing the nine cultures planned after Greece.

The deep dives

  • Deep dive I, the Trojan War (unit 10, 12 modules) and deep dive II, Alexander (unit 11, 8 modules) were added to the syllabus, bringing it to 70 modules.
  • Troy was written in two halves: 62 of 70 reports, about 197,000 words.
  • 24 October: Alexander, 11.1 to 11.8. The course was complete at 70 reports, about 219,500 words. A writer published 11.6 as a stray page.

Personal Mythology

  • The project was renamed and reframed as Personal Mythology, a personal journey towards understanding mythos and logos.
  • The "Next steps" page became the root home page: a mythos and logos introduction, "How every course works", Greece as course 1 and nine cultures coming soon.
  • A home icon was added to the top bar. The drawer became context-aware: courses on the home page, one course's units inside it.
  • Unit 12, a quality audit (12.1 to 12.5), was added to the syllabus, bringing it to 75 modules.
  • provenance.json records which model wrote each report: Fable 5.1 wrote 55, and Opus 5.5 wrote 15 (10.4, 10.7 to 10.12, all of unit 11). Each report shows its model at the foot.
  • The spacing rule: at least 6 px between stacked boxes, checked by a script on all 73 pages.
  • The source was packaged as a kit that rebuilds the site byte for byte, with the first handoff prompt, for Rome units 0 and 1.

Academy›Course A · Personal brand›How it is built
PERSONAL BRAND · HOW IT IS BUILT

How the course gets made

Method, model rules, and the pages that hold the briefs, the audits and the sources. Nothing here is course content.

Now in Academy

Since v7 (28 Oct 2026, A3) the two courses are read here, in Academy, with the history courses; the standalone hub is gone. Where the procedure below and the Project's method.md differ, method.md governs: the Project, the vault path, the audit cadence and the review model are set there. The text below is kept as the v5.1 record.

Build pages

MHow we buildprocedure, models, review rule1·2Build order with models0 units to goP·ABriefs · course A0 openAUAudits and audit promptsnext Audit 3 (DD-B3, A3, B5, A4), Audit 4 (A5, B7, A7, A8), Audit 5 (B6, B8, DD-A1, DD-B1) and Audit 6, the final audit (DD-A2, DD-B2 and both courses read in full), owed to the Fable 5.1 stageBIBBibliography337 sourcesLOGChangelogv9.4✓How it was builtmodels, checks, audits, flags

Pick

B1 first, written by Opus 5.5 in a new chat in a new Project.

  • B1 makes every later brief cheaper; it is the cheapest unit.
  • A separate Project keeps 21 units out of the personal assistant.
  • Fable reads nothing until four units exist.

Override if: the brand course should move first; then A1 first, B1 second, same setup.

Procedure

  1. Make a Project ("Courses — Brand & AI"). Add to its knowledge: this page, the research report, the five notes files. Project instructions, three lines: who the reader is; this page holds every brief; each unit is written in full, not described.
  2. One chat per unit. Copy the unit's brief from its Briefs page, paste it into a new chat in that Project. Never reuse a chat across units; leaked context is how a course starts repeating itself.
  3. Save the output to the vault as courses/brand-ai/<code>-<slug>.md (e.g. B1-mental-model.md), same shape as the history course. Hand it to the assistant: it commits, adds the unit to Learn, moves the counters, adds a changelog line.
  4. Units that need live facts (A8, B2, B8, DD-A1, DD-B3) are written with web search on; the briefs say so. Everything else without.
  5. After every fourth unit, run the audit prompt from the Audits page. The first fires after B1.

Models

  • Writing: Opus 5.5 for all remaining units. The quality gap shows most on long-form synthesis, which is this.
  • The shipped deliverable is written in the same chat as its unit, never separately, so it inherits the unit's reasoning.
  • Reviewing: Fable 5.1, in the personal-assistant Project where this hub lives. One claim per section per unit checked against its source; more than one in ten fails → full audit.
  • Not Sonnet or Haiku for units or audits. They are the right tools for turning a finished unit into posts, a Shorts list or an email; that is B7's pipeline.

Review rule

After every four written units, across both courses, Fable 5.1 audits by stratified sample: one claim per section per unit, checked against its source; more than one in ten fails → full audit of that unit. Units written with live search (A2, A6, A9, A8, B2, B8, DD-B3) are re-checked for staleness six months after writing.

Audit 1 ran 28 Oct: B1 PASS; A2·A6·A9 REWRITE. The three were rewritten the same day from their briefs, each fresh-checked by Fable 5.1 (A9 2 errors, A2 3, A6 1; all fixed). B2 is next; every unit gets a fresh checker.

Academy›Course A · Personal brand›Build order
PERSONAL BRAND · BUILD ORDER

Building order, with models

Zero units remain. One row per unit in writing order; an audit row every fourth written unit. Opus 5.5 writes; Fable 5.1 audits. The last deep dive was briefed for Fable 5.1 because it reviews the chat history; Pedro set that exception aside on 31 Oct 2026 and it was written by Opus 5.5 with the past-chat tools. "Search" means the unit is written with web search on because its facts are current.

  • Why this order: B1–B4 first so every later brief is cheaper; A1 and A3 next because the Athens edit and the half-marathon season need the positioning and the journey system now; DD-B3 between them (added 28 Oct 2026) so B5, B7 and DD-B1 inherit the practitioners' methods it verifies; B5 beside A3 and B7 beside A5 because they build the same pipeline; A7, A8, B6, B8 once there is a channel to measure; deep dives last, DD-B2 last of all.
  • Cadence: one unit per 3–4 days is the sustainable rate alongside the half-marathon block; that is 10–11 weeks to the end, with audits adding an evening each.
  • Audit gate: no new unit starts while an audit verdict is REWRITE on an earlier one.

Academy›Course A · Personal brand›Audits
PERSONAL BRAND · AUDITS

Fable audit log and prompts

One audit run. Each audit records: units sampled, claims checked, failures, verdict (pass / full audit / rewrite), and what changed.

Audit log

2026-10-29Audit 2 (Fable 5.1, stratified sample, live search). Units: B2, B3, B4, A1. B2·B3·B4·A1: PASS, all four: 28 claims sampled, 96 checked in all; 0 sampled FAIL; 1 incidental FAIL (B2 §7: the Claude Code post's seven-times caption is not one of its charts labelled illustrative); 10 STALE, all B2, one cause: Artificial Analysis moved to Intelligence Index v5.0 on 27 Oct, the day before B2 read its leaderboard; 2 unverifiable (Social Blade fetch, one rate-limited YouTube page). No full audit triggered. Errata applied the same day: B2-1 to B2-25 (every v5.0 figure, the readings and ratios recomputed, the miscite corrected, the v5.0 announcement added to Sources; every pick in the routing table unchanged) and A1-1 (§4 option 3: the handle @apcanario is already his; backlog P33). Report: audits/brand-ai/audit-2.md.
2026-10-28Audit 1 (Fable 5.1, stratified sample, then full audit). Units: A2·A6·A9 (the audience-growth research) and B1. B1: PASS, 7 of 7 sampled claims hold, deliverable present. A2·A6·A9: sample 1 of 8 FAIL → full audit: 91 claims, 23 FAIL, 0 STALE, nothing invented, no conclusion flips → REWRITE, because the three deliverables (audience profile and allocation; playbook and metrics sheet; benchmarks table and checkpoints) do not exist and two table rows rest on figures no vidIQ page contains. Pattern: qualifiers the source does not carry. One non-accuracy item: a cited creator profile is a minor's; the name comes out. Consequences: B2 waits for the rewrite; every later Brand + AI unit gets a fresh checker (method §1 override). Report: audits/brand-ai/audit-1.html. The three fresh checks that followed (A9 2 errors, 15 should-fix, 8 minor; A2 3/13/11; A6 1/9/7; all applied) are filed as audits/brand-ai/fresh-check-A9.md, fresh-check-A2.md and fresh-check-A6.md; B2's (3 errors, 10 should-fix, 14 minor) as fresh-check-B2.md; A1's (1 error, 4 should-fix, 6 minor) as fresh-check-A1.md, and its §6 revision's (1 error, 1 should-fix, 3 minor) as fresh-check-A1-s6.md; B3's (1 error, 4 should-fix, 9 minor) as fresh-check-B3.md; B4's (0 errors, 7 should-fix, 9 minor) as fresh-check-B4.md.

Audit prompts

Run in the personal-assistant Project with Fable 5.1, on command, after every fourth written unit. Paste the units or point at their vault paths.

Stratified-sample audit (every four units)
You are auditing four units of a self-study course written by Opus 5.5 for one reader. Units: {list the four unit codes and their vault paths}.

Method, in this order:
1. For each unit, list its sections. From every section draw one claim that carries a number, a named source, a mechanism or a recommendation. That is the sample; record it before checking anything.
2. Check each sampled claim against the source the unit cites: open the source; confirm the figure, the date, the sample size and that the claim is what the source says, not a stretch of it. Mark each claim PASS, FAIL (wrong, unsupported, miscited, or invented) or STALE (true when written, superseded now, with the newer figure).
3. For each unit compute the failure rate. If more than one claim in ten is FAIL, audit that unit in full: every cited claim, same procedure.
4. Check the deliverable: it exists in full inside the unit, it follows from the unit's evidence, and it is usable without reading the unit.
5. Check against the course goal and sourcing standard (in the unit's preamble): does the unit move the reader toward the stated outcomes, and is anything in it advice without evidence?

Output: a table per unit (section, claim, source, verdict, note); the failure rate; a verdict per unit (PASS / FULL AUDIT RUN, result / REWRITE with the sections named); the three most consequential errors across the four units in plain language; and one line each for the bibliography (new sources to add, sources to mark failed) and the changelog. Do not fix the units; report.
Staleness check (six months after a live-search unit)
You are re-checking one unit written with live search six months ago: {unit code, vault path, date written}. Search again, today, for every fact in it that can change: platform rules and thresholds, prices and plan limits, model lineups, benchmark figures, regulatory status, the numbers attached to named creators. For each: the value in the unit, the value now, the source and date for the new value, and whether the unit's conclusion still holds. End with a verdict: CURRENT / PATCH (list the lines to change) / REWRITE (say which sections). Do not rewrite; report.

Academy›Course B · Building with AI›How it is built
BUILDING WITH AI · HOW IT IS BUILT

How the course gets made

Method, model rules, and the pages that hold the briefs, the audits and the sources. Nothing here is course content.

Now in Academy

Since v7 (28 Oct 2026, A3) the two courses are read here, in Academy, with the history courses; the standalone hub is gone. Where the procedure below and the Project's method.md differ, method.md governs: the Project, the vault path, the audit cadence and the review model are set there. The text below is kept as the v5.1 record.

Build pages

MHow we buildprocedure, models, review rule1·2Build order with models0 units to goP·BBriefs · course B0 openAUAudits and audit promptsnext Audit 3 (DD-B3, A3, B5, A4), Audit 4 (A5, B7, A7, A8), Audit 5 (B6, B8, DD-A1, DD-B1) and Audit 6, the final audit (DD-A2, DD-B2 and both courses read in full), owed to the Fable 5.1 stageBIBBibliography200 sourcesLOGChangelogv9.4✓How it was builtmodels, checks, audits, flags

Pick

B1 first, written by Opus 5.5 in a new chat in a new Project.

  • B1 makes every later brief cheaper; it is the cheapest unit.
  • A separate Project keeps 21 units out of the personal assistant.
  • Fable reads nothing until four units exist.

Override if: the brand course should move first; then A1 first, B1 second, same setup.

Procedure

  1. Make a Project ("Courses — Brand & AI"). Add to its knowledge: this page, the research report, the five notes files. Project instructions, three lines: who the reader is; this page holds every brief; each unit is written in full, not described.
  2. One chat per unit. Copy the unit's brief from its Briefs page, paste it into a new chat in that Project. Never reuse a chat across units; leaked context is how a course starts repeating itself.
  3. Save the output to the vault as courses/brand-ai/<code>-<slug>.md (e.g. B1-mental-model.md), same shape as the history course. Hand it to the assistant: it commits, adds the unit to Learn, moves the counters, adds a changelog line.
  4. Units that need live facts (A8, B2, B8, DD-A1, DD-B3) are written with web search on; the briefs say so. Everything else without.
  5. After every fourth unit, run the audit prompt from the Audits page. The first fires after B1.

Models

  • Writing: Opus 5.5 for all remaining units. The quality gap shows most on long-form synthesis, which is this.
  • The shipped deliverable is written in the same chat as its unit, never separately, so it inherits the unit's reasoning.
  • Reviewing: Fable 5.1, in the personal-assistant Project where this hub lives. One claim per section per unit checked against its source; more than one in ten fails → full audit.
  • Not Sonnet or Haiku for units or audits. They are the right tools for turning a finished unit into posts, a Shorts list or an email; that is B7's pipeline.

Review rule

After every four written units, across both courses, Fable 5.1 audits by stratified sample: one claim per section per unit, checked against its source; more than one in ten fails → full audit of that unit. Units written with live search (A2, A6, A9, A8, B2, B8, DD-B3) are re-checked for staleness six months after writing.

Audit 1 ran 28 Oct: B1 PASS; A2·A6·A9 REWRITE. The three were rewritten the same day from their briefs, each fresh-checked by Fable 5.1 (A9 2 errors, A2 3, A6 1; all fixed). B2 is next; every unit gets a fresh checker.

BUILDING WITH AI · BUILD ORDER

Building order, with models

Zero units remain. One row per unit in writing order; an audit row every fourth written unit. Opus 5.5 writes; Fable 5.1 audits. The last deep dive was briefed for Fable 5.1 because it reviews the chat history; Pedro set that exception aside on 31 Oct 2026 and it was written by Opus 5.5 with the past-chat tools. "Search" means the unit is written with web search on because its facts are current.

  • Why this order: B1–B4 first so every later brief is cheaper; A1 and A3 next because the Athens edit and the half-marathon season need the positioning and the journey system now; DD-B3 between them (added 28 Oct 2026) so B5, B7 and DD-B1 inherit the practitioners' methods it verifies; B5 beside A3 and B7 beside A5 because they build the same pipeline; A7, A8, B6, B8 once there is a channel to measure; deep dives last, DD-B2 last of all.
  • Cadence: one unit per 3–4 days is the sustainable rate alongside the half-marathon block; that is 10–11 weeks to the end, with audits adding an evening each.
  • Audit gate: no new unit starts while an audit verdict is REWRITE on an earlier one.

Academy›Course B · Building with AI›Audits
BUILDING WITH AI · AUDITS

Fable audit log and prompts

One audit run. Each audit records: units sampled, claims checked, failures, verdict (pass / full audit / rewrite), and what changed.

Audit log

2026-10-29Audit 2 (Fable 5.1, stratified sample, live search). Units: B2, B3, B4, A1. B2·B3·B4·A1: PASS, all four: 28 claims sampled, 96 checked in all; 0 sampled FAIL; 1 incidental FAIL (B2 §7: the Claude Code post's seven-times caption is not one of its charts labelled illustrative); 10 STALE, all B2, one cause: Artificial Analysis moved to Intelligence Index v5.0 on 27 Oct, the day before B2 read its leaderboard; 2 unverifiable (Social Blade fetch, one rate-limited YouTube page). No full audit triggered. Errata applied the same day: B2-1 to B2-25 (every v5.0 figure, the readings and ratios recomputed, the miscite corrected, the v5.0 announcement added to Sources; every pick in the routing table unchanged) and A1-1 (§4 option 3: the handle @apcanario is already his; backlog P33). Report: audits/brand-ai/audit-2.md.
2026-10-28Audit 1 (Fable 5.1, stratified sample, then full audit). Units: A2·A6·A9 (the audience-growth research) and B1. B1: PASS, 7 of 7 sampled claims hold, deliverable present. A2·A6·A9: sample 1 of 8 FAIL → full audit: 91 claims, 23 FAIL, 0 STALE, nothing invented, no conclusion flips → REWRITE, because the three deliverables (audience profile and allocation; playbook and metrics sheet; benchmarks table and checkpoints) do not exist and two table rows rest on figures no vidIQ page contains. Pattern: qualifiers the source does not carry. One non-accuracy item: a cited creator profile is a minor's; the name comes out. Consequences: B2 waits for the rewrite; every later Brand + AI unit gets a fresh checker (method §1 override). Report: audits/brand-ai/audit-1.html. The three fresh checks that followed (A9 2 errors, 15 should-fix, 8 minor; A2 3/13/11; A6 1/9/7; all applied) are filed as audits/brand-ai/fresh-check-A9.md, fresh-check-A2.md and fresh-check-A6.md; B2's (3 errors, 10 should-fix, 14 minor) as fresh-check-B2.md; A1's (1 error, 4 should-fix, 6 minor) as fresh-check-A1.md, and its §6 revision's (1 error, 1 should-fix, 3 minor) as fresh-check-A1-s6.md; B3's (1 error, 4 should-fix, 9 minor) as fresh-check-B3.md; B4's (0 errors, 7 should-fix, 9 minor) as fresh-check-B4.md.

Audit prompts

Run in the personal-assistant Project with Fable 5.1, on command, after every fourth written unit. Paste the units or point at their vault paths.

Stratified-sample audit (every four units)
You are auditing four units of a self-study course written by Opus 5.5 for one reader. Units: {list the four unit codes and their vault paths}.

Method, in this order:
1. For each unit, list its sections. From every section draw one claim that carries a number, a named source, a mechanism or a recommendation. That is the sample; record it before checking anything.
2. Check each sampled claim against the source the unit cites: open the source; confirm the figure, the date, the sample size and that the claim is what the source says, not a stretch of it. Mark each claim PASS, FAIL (wrong, unsupported, miscited, or invented) or STALE (true when written, superseded now, with the newer figure).
3. For each unit compute the failure rate. If more than one claim in ten is FAIL, audit that unit in full: every cited claim, same procedure.
4. Check the deliverable: it exists in full inside the unit, it follows from the unit's evidence, and it is usable without reading the unit.
5. Check against the course goal and sourcing standard (in the unit's preamble): does the unit move the reader toward the stated outcomes, and is anything in it advice without evidence?

Output: a table per unit (section, claim, source, verdict, note); the failure rate; a verdict per unit (PASS / FULL AUDIT RUN, result / REWRITE with the sections named); the three most consequential errors across the four units in plain language; and one line each for the bibliography (new sources to add, sources to mark failed) and the changelog. Do not fix the units; report.
Staleness check (six months after a live-search unit)
You are re-checking one unit written with live search six months ago: {unit code, vault path, date written}. Search again, today, for every fact in it that can change: platform rules and thresholds, prices and plan limits, model lineups, benchmark figures, regulatory status, the numbers attached to named creators. For each: the value in the unit, the value now, the source and date for the new value, and whether the unit's conclusion still holds. End with a verdict: CURRENT / PATCH (list the lines to change) / REWRITE (say which sections). Do not rewrite; report.

Academy›Course IA · Information architecture›How it is built
INFORMATION ARCHITECTURE · HOW IT IS BUILT

How the course gets made

Method, model rules, and the pages that hold the briefs, the audits and the sources. Nothing here is course content.

Build pages

MHow we buildprocedure, models, review rule1·2Build order with models8 units to goP·IABriefs · course IA8 openAUAudits and audit promptsnext Audit 1 (4 of 4: IA1, IA2, IA3, IA4), owed to the Fable stage (P49); Audit 2 at 3 of 4 (IA5, IA6, IA7); neither gates a unitBIBBibliography126 sourcesLOGChangelogv10✓How it was builtmodels, checks, audits, flags

Pick

IA1 first, once the kit can draw the course's figures.

  • IA2 fixes the notation; IA1 only needs the kinds to exist.
  • The four levels (IA5 to IA8) are the course; everything before them is what they rest on.
  • Deep dives last, DD-IA1 and DD-IA2 with search on.

Override if: the figure kinds take more than one chat to add; then IA1 and IA2 are written with the four existing kinds and their figures redrawn when the kinds land.

Procedure

  1. One chat per unit, in the Learning Project. Copy the unit's brief from this course's Briefs page and paste it into a new chat; never reuse a chat across units. An isolated agent context counts as a chat (method.md §4, P31).
  2. The unit is written in full with its deliverable and its figures, each figure a fenced viz block of a kind kit/viz.py can draw. Every Perqo fact the unit invents is labelled and kept consistent with the Perqo facts already filed.
  3. A fresh checker in a new chat (Fable 5.1, skills/audit-units §3b) checks every cited claim and every figure's content against the unit, and the deliverable against the brief; findings are applied as exact replacements and the report filed as audits/ux/fresh-check-<code>.md.
  4. File the unit at courses/ux/<code>-<slug>.md with the front matter of method.md §7 (skills/file-unit); update this data file, rebuild, verify, publish Academy.
  5. After every fourth unit, the stratified-sample audit from the Audits page. The first fires after IA4.

Models

  • Writing: Opus 5.5 for every unit. The deliverable is written in the same chat as its unit.
  • Checking and auditing: Fable 5.1, a fresh context each time, with live search for the audits and for DD-IA1 and DD-IA2.
  • Not Sonnet or Haiku for units or audits.

Review rule

After every four written units, Fable 5.1 audits by stratified sample: one claim per section per unit, checked against its source; more than one in ten fails → full audit of that unit. Figures count as claims: a sampled figure is checked for whether its labels and numbers match the unit's sourced text. Units written with live search (DD-IA1, DD-IA2) are re-checked for staleness six months after writing.

Academy›Course IA · Information architecture›Build order
INFORMATION ARCHITECTURE · BUILD ORDER

Building order, with models

Eight units remain. One row per unit in writing order; an audit row every fourth written unit. Opus 5.5 writes; Fable 5.1 checks and audits. "Search" means the unit is written with web search on because its facts are current.

  • Why this order: Part I (IA1 to IA4) gives the definitions, the notation, the research base and the organisation systems every later figure uses; Part II (IA5 to IA8) builds Perqo one level at a time, each level reusing the previous one's objects and labels, so the levels are written in order; Part III (IA9 to IA12) places the finished IA in the cycle and the business and ends with the missing-inputs playbook, which reuses IA7's decisions as its register; the deep dives last, the comparables and the cost one with search on.
  • Before IA1: the figure kinds the course needs (a sitemap and object map with notes, a decision tree with labelled branches, a matrix, a state diagram, a swimlane map, a wireflow) are added to kit/viz.py and design/theme.css §4i as their own backlog row; no unit is written until they draw.
  • Audit gate: no new unit starts while an audit verdict is REWRITE on an earlier one.

Academy›Course IA · Information architecture›Audits
INFORMATION ARCHITECTURE · AUDITS

Fable audit log and prompts

No audit yet. Each audit records: units sampled, claims checked, failures, verdict (pass / full audit / rewrite), and what changed.

Audit log

Audit prompts

Run in the Learning Project with Fable 5.1, on command, after every fourth written unit. Paste the units or point at their vault paths. The prompts are method.md §6, verbatim; for this course a sampled figure is a sampled claim.

Stratified-sample audit (every four units)
You are auditing four units of a self-study course written by Opus 5.5 for one reader. Units: {list the four unit codes and their vault paths}.

Method, in this order:
1. For each unit, list its sections. From every section draw one claim that carries a number, a named source, a mechanism or a recommendation. That is the sample; record it before checking anything.
2. Check each sampled claim against the source the unit cites: open the source; confirm the figure, the date, the sample size and that the claim is what the source says, not a stretch of it. Mark each claim PASS, FAIL (wrong, unsupported, miscited, or invented) or STALE (true when written, superseded now, with the newer figure).
3. For each unit compute the failure rate. If more than one claim in ten is FAIL, audit that unit in full: every cited claim, same procedure.
4. Check the deliverable: it exists in full inside the unit, it follows from the unit's evidence, and it is usable without reading the unit.
5. Check against the course goal and sourcing standard (in the unit's preamble): does the unit move the reader toward the stated outcomes, and is anything in it advice without evidence?

Output: a table per unit (section, claim, source, verdict, note); the failure rate; a verdict per unit (PASS / FULL AUDIT RUN, result / REWRITE with the sections named); the three most consequential errors across the four units in plain language; and one line each for the bibliography (new sources to add, sources to mark failed) and the changelog. Do not fix the units; report.
Staleness check (six months after a live-search unit)
You are re-checking one unit written with live search six months ago: {unit code, vault path, date written}. Search again, today, for every fact in it that can change: platform rules and thresholds, prices and plan limits, model lineups, benchmark figures, regulatory status, the numbers attached to named creators. For each: the value in the unit, the value now, the source and date for the new value, and whether the unit's conclusion still holds. End with a verdict: CURRENT / PATCH (list the lines to change) / REWRITE (say which sections). Do not rewrite; report.
